\displaystyle\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)\left(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathfrak{b}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\xi)T_{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\eta)}T_{1-Y}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathfrak{b}}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\zeta)T_{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\xi)}T_{1-Y}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\eta)\left(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{b}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{b}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\xi+\eta)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)\mathfrak{b}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\right).

By definition,
|\xi|\ll|\eta|
,
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)\approx\chi_{1}(\xi,\xi+\eta)
, and
\xi
,
\eta
are negative.
One can compute the symbol
\mathfrak{b}^{\sharp}_{lh}
, which is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)=

\displaystyle\left(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathfrak{b}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)+(1+\eta^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{b}^{a}_{lh}(\xi,\xi+\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\dfrac{9\xi^{3}+28\xi^{2}\eta+27\xi\eta^{2}+4\eta^{3}}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle+(1+\eta^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\dfrac{2\xi^{3}-3\xi^{2}(\xi+\eta)+7\xi(\xi+\eta)^{2}-14(\xi+\eta)^{3}}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+\frac{1}{2}}+\text{lower order terms of }\eta.

The leading part of the energy correction
I_{1}^{s}
is

\tilde{I}_{1}^{s}=-2\Re\int\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\cdot\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\,d\alpha.

(2)

The cubic integrals that involve
T_{\widehat{R}}\widehat{R}
or
T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}}\bar{\widehat{R}}
.
This part of integrals is given by

\displaystyle I_{2}^{s}=

\displaystyle\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)}T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})^{2}(1-Y)}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{\mathfrak{c}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\xi)T_{\widehat{R}(\eta)}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\zeta)T_{\widehat{R}(\xi)}T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}(1-\bar{Y})}\widehat{R}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathfrak{c}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)}T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{a}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)}T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)^{2}}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)\mathfrak{c}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\right).

Here, we use the fact
J^{-\frac{3}{2}}J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})^{2}(1-Y)=(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})
to simplify the para-coefficients.
The symbol
\mathfrak{c}^{\sharp}_{lh}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{\sharp}_{lh}=

\displaystyle(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{\mathfrak{c}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)+(1+\eta^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{a}^{a}_{lh}(\xi,\xi+\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\dfrac{3\xi^{3}+6\xi^{2}\eta+11\xi\eta^{2}+6\eta^{3}}{\xi\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle-(1+\eta^{2})^{s}\dfrac{6\xi^{4}-15\xi^{3}(\xi+\eta)+20\xi^{2}(\xi+\eta)^{2}-20\xi(\xi+\eta)^{3}+12(\xi+\eta)^{4}}{2\xi\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}+\text{lower order terms of }\eta,

since
\xi,\eta<0
, and
|\eta|^{2s+1}
terms get canceled.
Hence, the leading part of the energy correction
I_{2}^{s}
is

\tilde{I}_{2}^{s}=-\frac{2}{3}\Re\int\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\cdot\langle D\rangle^{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)^{2}}\bar{R}\,d\alpha.

(3)

The cubic integrals that involve
T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}}\widehat{R}
or
T_{\widehat{R}}\bar{\widehat{R}}
.
This part of integral is given by

\displaystyle I_{3}^{s}=

\displaystyle\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\xi)T_{\widehat{R}(\eta)}T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{\mathfrak{c}}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)}T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}R(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathfrak{c}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\zeta)T_{\widehat{R}(\xi)}T_{1-Y}\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{a}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\right).

Here we use the fact that
J^{-\frac{3}{2}}J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})=1-Y
.
The symbol
\mathfrak{a}^{\sharp}_{lh}
is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{\sharp}_{lh}=

\displaystyle(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)+(1+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathfrak{c}^{a}_{lh}(\xi,\xi+\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\dfrac{-9\xi^{4}-\xi^{3}\eta+26\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+28\xi\eta^{3}+12\eta^{4}}{2\xi\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle+(1+\eta^{2})^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\dfrac{(\xi+\eta)(5\xi^{2}-7\xi(\xi+\eta)+6(\xi+\eta)^{2})}{\xi\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}+\text{lower order terms of }\eta.

Hence, the leading part of the energy correction
I_{3}^{s}
is

\tilde{I}_{3}^{s}=\frac{2}{3}\Re\int\langle D\rangle^{s}R\cdot\langle D\rangle^{s}T_{\bar{R}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\,d\alpha.

(4)

The rest part of the cubic integrals, where
R
or
\bar{R}
does not have lowest frequency.
It is given by

\displaystyle I_{4}^{s}=

\displaystyle\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\xi)T_{{\mathbf{W}}(\eta)}T_{1-Y}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\zeta)T_{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}(\xi)}T_{1-Y}\widehat{R}(\eta)(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{d}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\xi)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\eta)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\widehat{R}(\zeta)(1+\xi^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{d}^{a}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)\,d\xi d\eta.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\widehat{R}(\zeta)T_{\bar{\widehat{{\mathbf{W}}}}(\xi)}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\widehat{R}}(\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)\,d\xi d\eta+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\right).

The symbol
\mathfrak{d}^{\sharp}_{lh}
is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{\sharp}_{lh}=

\displaystyle(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}^{h}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)+(1+\eta^{2})^{s}\mathfrak{d}^{\sharp}_{lh}(\xi,\xi+\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}\dfrac{6\xi^{3}+15\xi^{2}\eta+7\xi\eta^{2}-4\eta^{3}}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle+(1+\eta^{2})^{s}\dfrac{8\xi^{3}-20\xi^{2}(\xi+\eta)+23\xi(\xi+\eta)^{2}-14(\xi+\eta)^{3}}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1+\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{s}+\text{lower order terms of }\eta.

The leading part of the energy correction
I_{4}^{s}
is

\tilde{I}_{4}^{s}=-2\Re\int\langle D\rangle^{s}R\cdot\langle D\rangle^{s}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{R}\,d\alpha.

We still need to construct the paradifferential modified energy
I_{5}^{s}
to remove the remaining non-perturbative cubic energy.
This part of non-perturbative cubic integrals include the first term on the right-hand side of (
3.8
) and the sub-leading part of the second integral of (
3.8
).
These non-perturbative cubic integrals need
\frac{1}{2}
less derivative on the high-frequency factor to be bounded by the right hand side of (
1.8
).
One can try to find cubic corrections
I_{5}^{s}
of the following type:

I^{s}_{5}=\Re\int L^{hhl}_{2s+1}({\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}}+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L^{hhl}_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}({\mathbf{W}},\bar{R},R+\bar{R})\,d\alpha,

where
L^{hhl}_{s}
is some trilinear form where each of its arguments has relatively high, high, and low frequencies respectively, and
s
derivatives in total are distributed to two variables with high frequencies.
Let the frequencies of the first and the third (or the second and the third) factor of
L^{hhl}_{2s+1}
be
\xi
and
\eta
.
Then similar to the computation for the symbols of
\mathfrak{a}^{\sharp}_{lh}
, the denominator of the symbols of these trilinear forms have a factor
9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2}
, which cannot be zero unless
\xi=\eta=0
.
This indicates that the symbols of
L^{hhl}_{s}
is bounded.

When taking the time derivative of each factor of cubic energy corrections
I^{s}_{5}
, a leading order
\frac{3}{2}
term is generated and cancels a part of the non-perturbative cubic integral terms.
This process leaves behind sub-leading terms of order
1
, featuring
\frac{1}{2}
fewer total derivatives compared to the non-perturbative cubic integral terms.
This shows that the remaining cubic terms are perturbative after cancellation.

The five parts of paradifferential modified energy satisfy the energy equivalence property

|I_{1}^{s}|+|I_{2}^{s}|+|I_{3}^{s}|+|I_{4}^{s}|+|I_{5}^{s}|\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}.

Writing

E^{(\leq 3)}_{s}=E^{1}_{s}({\mathbf{W}}+{\mathbf{W}}_{[2]}^{hh},R+R_{[2]}^{hh})+I_{1}^{s}+I_{2}^{s}+I_{3}^{s}+I_{4}^{s}+I_{5}^{s},

then the energy
E^{(\leq 3)}_{s}
satisfies the norm equivalence property (
1.7
), and its time derivative has no non-perturbative cubic integral terms,

\frac{d}{dt}E^{(\leq 3)}_{s}=\text{non-perturbative quartic integral terms}+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\right).

3.4.
Quartic modified energy

Having constructed the almost balanced cubic modified energy, we now build the quartic modified energy to eliminate the non-perturbative quartic integral terms in
\frac{d}{dt}E^{(\leq 3)}_{s}
. A similar computation was carried out in
[
23
]
without a paradifferential reduction.

The non-perturbative quartic integral terms can be separated into two parts:

•

The time derivative acts on the para-coefficients, resulting in an additional
T_{R_{\alpha}}
or
T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}
.
When constructing the cubic paradifferential modified energy, para-coefficients such as
T_{1-Y}
,
T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}
and so on are treated as constants.
Upon taking the time derivatives on these para-coefficients, the leading terms of their time derivatives are para-coefficients
T_{R_{\alpha}}
or
T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}
.

•

The transport derivative
T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}
acts on the main factors of low-high cubic energy, introducing a non-perturbative quartic integral.
When the time derivative acts on the main factors of low-high cubic energy, the leading parts of order
\frac{3}{2}
interact with other terms to eliminate the non-perturbative cubic integrals.
The sub-leading factors of order
1
, namely,
T_{b}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}
,
T_{b}R_{\alpha}
,
T_{b}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}
or
T_{b}\bar{R}_{\alpha}
produce new non-perturbative quartic integral terms.

For the second case, we can always use integration by parts to shift the
\alpha
-derivative from the high-frequency variables to para-coefficients of low frequencies.
For instance, consider one of the integral term

\displaystyle\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{b}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=-\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{b}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\cdot T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{b}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}+T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\cdot T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{b_{\alpha}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\text{perturbative integrals},

using integration by parts.
The first term on the right will cancel with another integral

\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}T_{b}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}\langle D\rangle^{s+{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\,d\alpha

at the leading order.
For the remaining two integral terms, the
\alpha
-derivatives are on the variables with low frequencies.

Another observation is that two variables at low frequencies cannot have comparable frequencies, and
\alpha
-derivatives always fall on variables with relatively high frequencies.
This is because if
\alpha
-derivatives fall on variables with the lowest or comparable frequencies, one can shift half a derivative to the variables with medium frequencies and the quartic integral terms are perturbative.
Let the frequencies of four arguments of quartic integrals be
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}
, then one can always arrange such that,

(3.9)

0<|\xi_{1}|\ll|\xi_{3}|\ll|\xi_{2}|\approx|\xi_{4}|.

Note that these non-perturbative quartic integral terms need
\frac{1}{2}
less derivative to be perturbative.
More precisely, the quartic integral terms are of the following type:

\displaystyle\Re

\displaystyle\int L_{2s+1}^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}},R_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L_{2s+1}^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L_{2s+1}^{lhmh}(R,{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L_{2s+1}^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Re

\displaystyle\int L_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},R_{\alpha},\bar{R})+L_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{R})+L_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}^{lhmh}(R,{\mathbf{W}},\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{R})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}^{lhmh}(\bar{R},R,\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L_{2s+\frac{1}{2}}^{lhmh}(R,R,\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Re

\displaystyle\int L_{2s}^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R,\bar{R}_{\alpha},\bar{R})+L_{2s}^{lhmh}(R,R,\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},\bar{R})+L_{2s}^{lhmh}(\bar{R},R,\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},\bar{R})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L_{2s}^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R,R_{\alpha},\bar{R})\,d\alpha.

Here
L^{lhmh}_{s}
is some quadrilinear form where each of its arguments has relatively low, high, medium, and high frequencies respectively, and
s
derivatives in total are distributed to two variables with high frequencies.
The
\alpha
-derivatives always fall on variables with medium frequencies because of the observation we made above.

To eliminate these non-perturbative quartic terms, we consider the following quartic integral corrections:

\displaystyle\Re\int

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},R,\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R,R)+L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}},R)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},R,\bar{R})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{R},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{R},R)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{R},\bar{R})+L^{lhmh}({\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}(R,{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{R})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},R,R,\bar{R})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},{\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{R})+L^{lhmh}(\bar{R},R,\bar{R},\bar{R})+L^{lhmh}(R,R,\bar{R},\bar{R})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{lhmh}({\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},\bar{R},R)\,d\alpha.

We need to solve two
8\times 8
linear systems to determine
16
symbols for quadrilinear forms.
The computations are long but are just solving linear systems.
The detailed analysis are thus omitted, and we refer interested readers to Section
3.2
in
[
23
]
for a similar computation for cubic normal form corrections.
Here we briefly discuss why these quartic energies are bounded.

Consider just the leading parts of the water wave system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=-R_{\alpha}&\\
R_{t}=-i{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}.&\end{array}\right.

Eliminating the
R
(or
{\mathbf{W}}
) variable yields the scalar equation

(\partial_{t}^{2}+i\partial_{\alpha}^{3}){\mathbf{W}}=0\left(\text{or }(\partial_{t}^{2}+i\partial_{\alpha}^{3})R=0\right),

which then gives the linear dispersion relation

\tau=\pm|\xi|^{\frac{3}{2}},\quad\xi<0.

The resonance condition

|\xi_{1}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{3}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{4}|^{\frac{3}{2}}=0

is satisfied if and only if there are two pairs of equal frequencies and matching signs in the last relation, which is not possible since the frequencies satisfy (
3.9
).
This indicates that the interactions are non-resonant and symbols of quadrilinear forms cannot be unbounded.

The corresponding quartic energy corrections satisfy the norm equivalence property.
Upon applying the time derivative to each factor of quartic energy corrections, a leading order
\frac{3}{2}
term is generated and cancels a part of the non-perturbative quartic integral terms.
This process leaves behind sub-leading terms of order
1
, featuring
\frac{1}{2}
fewer total derivatives compared to the non-perturbative quartic integral terms.
Since these non-perturbative quartic integral terms have
\frac{1}{2}
more derivatives to be perturbative, the remaining quartic and higher integral terms after cancellation are perturbative, obviating the need for further quintic energy correction.

To conclude this section, let
E_{s}^{(4)}
be the quartic energy correction constructed above, and

E_{s}:=E^{(\leq 3)}_{s}+E_{s}^{(4)}=E^{1}_{s}({\mathbf{W}}+{\mathbf{W}}_{[2]}^{hh},R+R_{[2]}^{hh})+I_{1}^{s}+I_{2}^{s}+I_{3}^{s}+I_{4}^{s}+I_{5}^{s}+E_{s}^{(4)}.

Then the paradifferential modified energy
E_{s}
satisfies the norm equivalence (
1.7
) and modified energy estiamte (
1.8
).
This finishes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

4.
Estimate for the linearized equations

In this section, we derive the balanced cubic modified energy estimate for the linearized water wave system.
Let the solutions for the linearized
water wave equations around a solution
(W,Q)
to the system (
1.1
) by
(w,q)
and
r:=q-Rw
.
It is computed in Section 6 of
[
23
]
that the linearized unknowns
(w,r)
solve the system

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\partial_{t}+\mathfrak{M}_{b}\partial_{\alpha})w+\mathbf{P}\left[\frac{r_{\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\right]+\mathbf{P}b_{\alpha}w=\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r)-T_{w}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]-\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]))&\\
(\partial_{t}+\mathfrak{M}_{b}\partial_{\alpha})r+i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Lw}{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\right]-i{\mathbf{P}}[\frac{gw}{1+{\mathbf{W}}}]=\mathbf{P}\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r)+i{\mathbf{P}}[\frac{aw}{1+{\mathbf{W}}}]-iT_{1-Y}T_{a}w.&\end{array}\right.

Here
\mathfrak{M}_{b}f={\mathbf{P}}[bf]
, and the self-adjoint operator
L
is defined in (
2.1
).
The source terms
\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r),\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r)
are given by

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r)

\displaystyle=(1+\mathbf{W})(\mathbf{P}\bar{m}+\bar{\mathbf{P}}m)+w\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]-T_{{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]}w,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathbf{P}}n-\mathbf{P}\bar{n}+i{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}+iT_{1-Y}T_{a}w,

where

\displaystyle m:=\frac{r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w}{J}+\frac{\bar{R}w_{\alpha}}{(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}},\qquad n:=\frac{\bar{R}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)}{1+\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle p:=\dfrac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}-\left(\dfrac{2{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}-\dfrac{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}.

In this section, we prove the following modified energy estimate for the linearized system (
4.1
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume that for some fixed
s>1
, on the time interval
I=[0,T]
for some time
T
,
N^{s}:=\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(I;\mathcal{H}^{s})}<\infty
, and
\mathcal{A}_{1}\lesssim 1
.
If
(w,r)
solve the linearized system (
4.1
) on
I=[0,T]
, there exists an energy functional
E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r)
such that on
I=[0,T]
, we have the following two properties:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r)\approx_{{\mathcal{A}_{1}}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(2)

Energy estimate:

\frac{d}{dt}E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r)\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{1}},N^{s}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r).

From the linearized equations (
4.1
) we obtain the corresponding paradifferential flow

(4.3)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w=0&\\
T_{D_{t}}r+iT_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w-igT_{1-Y}w=0,&\end{array}\right.

where
\mathcal{L}:=\partial_{\alpha}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}
is the leading paradifferential term of the operator
L
.

\mathcal{L}
is also a self-adjoint operator.
Here in the first equation of the paradifferential flow we change
T_{{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha}}w
to
{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w
so that
T_{b_{\alpha}}
is a real para-coefficient.

The linearized equations (
4.1
) can be rewritten in the paradifferential form

(4.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w=\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}(w,r)&\\
T_{D_{t}}r+iT_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w-igT_{1-Y}w=\mathcal{K}^{\sharp}(w,r),&\end{array}\right.

where the source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
are given by

\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}={\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}+\mathcal{G}_{1}),\qquad\mathcal{K}^{\sharp}={\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}+\mathcal{K}_{1}),

with
(\mathcal{G}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{0})
are as (
4.2
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}=

\displaystyle\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})-(T_{w_{\alpha}}b+\Pi(w_{\alpha},b))-T_{w}b_{\alpha}-\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha})-T_{w}(R\bar{Y}_{\alpha})-\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}])

\displaystyle+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}b_{\alpha}-{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha}}w,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}=

\displaystyle-(T_{r_{\alpha}}b+\Pi(r_{\alpha},b))+i(T_{Lw}Y+\Pi(Lw,Y))-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w_{\alpha}}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}-1)-T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c

\displaystyle-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w_{\alpha},J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)-T_{1-Y}\Pi(w_{\alpha},c)-T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha})+iT_{1-Y}T_{w}a

\displaystyle+iT_{1-Y}\Pi(w,a)-iT_{(g+a)w}Y-i\Pi((g+a)w,Y)-T_{1-Y}T_{c}w_{\alpha}-T_{1-Y}T_{{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}}w

are the paradifferential truncations.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
4.1
, whose proof is divided into five steps outlined below.

First, in Section
4.1
, we simplify and find out the leading terms of the source terms.
Then we use these source term bounds together with the linear part of the paradifferential equation (
4.4
) to obtain the leading terms of the para-material derivatives of
(w,r)
.
In the following, we write the perturbative source terms for
(G,K)
that satisfy

(4.5)

\|(G,K)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the second step, in Section
4.2
, we prove the energy estimate of the homogeneous paradifferential flow (
4.3
):

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\in L^{1}_{t}([0,T])
for some time
T>0
, then if
(w,r)
solve the homogeneous paradifferential system (
4.3
) on
[0,T]
, there exists an energy functional
E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w,r)
such that on
[0,T]
, we have the following two properties:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w,r)\approx_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

(2)

The time derivative of
E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w,r)
is bounded by

\frac{d}{dt}E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w,r)\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}.

Note that here the implicit constant only depends on
{\mathcal{A}_{0}}
, which is sharper compared to the implicit constant in Theorem
4.1
.
We will see that Theorem
4.1
follows from Proposition
4.1
as long as the source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
satisfy,

(4.6)

\|(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{1}},N^{s}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

In Section
4.3
and Section
4.4
, we take into account the nonlinear source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
on the right-hand side of (
4.4
).
Unfortunately, the source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
do not satisfy the bound (
4.6
), because of both the quadratic contributions and the
non-perturbative cubic contributions.

To eliminate these unfavorable source terms, we use the paradifferential normal form analysis to construct the modified normal form linear variables
(w_{NF},r_{NF})
.
However, unlike the pure gravity case
[
5
,
41
]
, though the balanced parts of the corrections are bounded, the low-high parts of the modified normal form variables
(w_{NF},r_{NF})
do not satisfy the bound

(4.7)

\|(w_{NF},r_{NF})-(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the third step, in Section
4.3
, we compute the normal form transformations that remove the balanced portion of the quadratic source terms.
This part of balanced normal form transformations satisfy the estimate (
4.7
).
Then for the fourth step in Section
4.4
, we compute the normal form transformation to eliminate the low-high portion of the source terms.
This part of low-high normal form transformations only satisfy the estimate

\|(w_{NF},r_{NF})-(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{N^{s}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Finally, in Section
4.5
, we construct the cubic normal forms to remove the remaining non-perturbative cubic source terms.
Combining all normal form transformations together, we construct the paradifferential modified energy
E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r):=E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w_{NF},r_{NF})
that finalizes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

4.1.
Para-material derivatives of
(w,r)

In this subsection, we first compute the leading terms of the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
,
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
Then we compute the leading terms of para-material derivatives of
(w,r)
.
We will also compute the non-perturbative quadratic terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
,
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.

We begin with the leading terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
have the decomposition

{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}=\mathcal{G}_{0,0}+G,\quad{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}=\mathcal{K}_{0,0}+K,

where
(\mathcal{G}_{0,0},\mathcal{K}_{0,0})
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0,0}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{w}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}]+{\mathbf{P}}[T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R],

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{0,0}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]-\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]+\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}].

({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
satisfy the bound

(4.8)

\|({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

and the remainder terms
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

This Lemma is similar to a corresponding computation obtained in the pure gravity water wave model
[
5
]
, though using a different control norm.

Proof.

We first consider the estimate for the source term
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}
.
Writing

m=|1-Y|^{2}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)+(1-Y)^{2}\bar{R}w_{\alpha},

we claim that

(4.9)

\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Note that the derivative in
m
is always applied to one of the holomorphic variables, which has to be at or below the frequency of the undifferentiated antiholomorphic variable under the projection
\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}
.
For instance, we can estimate

\displaystyle\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{Y}r_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}=\|[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{Y}]r_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|Y\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\|r\|_{H^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|r\|_{H^{{\frac{1}{2}}}},

\displaystyle\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{R}w_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}=\|[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{R}]w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|R\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

using the commutator estimate (
A.14
).
For other terms such as
\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{Y}R_{\alpha}w]
, some factors like
\bar{Y}R_{\alpha}
must be antiholomorphic.
We again use the commutator estimates (
A.14
), (
A.3
), and (
A.7
),

\displaystyle\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{Y}R_{\alpha}w]\|_{L^{2}}=\|[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{Y}R_{\alpha}]]w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{Y}R_{\alpha}]\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\|T_{R_{\alpha}}\bar{Y}\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}+\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},\bar{Y})\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\right)\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|R\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|Y\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}+\epsilon}_{*}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

Hence, the bound for
\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m
(
4.9
) is proved.
Using (
4.9
) and commutator estimates (
A.14
), (
A.15
),

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{W}}\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m)\|_{H^{1}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},{\mathbf{W}}]\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{C^{1}_{*}}\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}[w\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]]\|_{H^{1}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},w]\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{1}}\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|T_{{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]}w\|_{H^{1}}\leq\|w\|_{H^{1}}\|{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

The terms
{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}})\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m)
,
{\mathbf{P}}[w\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]]
and
T_{{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]}w
may be put into
G
.
Similarly,
{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}}){\mathbf{P}}\bar{m})=T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}+G
.
We write

{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}=-{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}\bar{w})Y]+{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{w}_{\alpha}R].

Akin to the proof of Lemma
5.3
in
[
5
]
, we can peel off frequency components to get

{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{w}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})Y]+{\mathbf{P}}[T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}R]+G.

Applying the para-associativity (
A.31
),
T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}
falls on the holomorphic function.
According to the relation (
2.7
), one can replace
T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y
by
T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}
.
Then, we get the expression of
\mathcal{G}_{0,0}
.

For the estimate for the second source term
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}
, a similar analysis gives the bound

\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}n\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Writing
n=(1-Y)\bar{R}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)
, so that by putting the perturbative components into the source term
K
,

{\mathbf{P}}\bar{n}={\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]+K.

Due to the bound for
a
(
2.2
),

\|iT_{1-Y}T_{a}w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|a\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

The term
iT_{1-Y}T_{a}w
belongs to
K
.

If only suffices to consider
i{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
.
We write

{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}={\mathbf{P}}[(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1)\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]-\frac{3}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}].

For the second term of
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
, we apply the commutator estimate (
A.14
) and the Moser type estimate (
A.12
),

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|[{\mathbf{P}},J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}]\bar{w}_{\alpha}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}]\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\|T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi((1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\right)\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

so that it is perturbative and can be put into the error term
K
.
For the first and the third term of
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}[(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1)\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]\|_{L^{2}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1]\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1\|_{C^{1}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]\|_{L^{2}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},J^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}]\bar{w}_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|J^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

These two terms satisfy the bound (
4.8
).
For the first term of
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
, we claim that

J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1=-\frac{1}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}+E,

where the error term
E
satisfies

\|E\|_{C_{*}^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|{\mathbf{W}}\|_{C^{1}_{*}}^{2}.

Indeed, we can write

J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1=f({\mathbf{W}},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}),\quad f(x,y)=(1+x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+y)^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1.

Direct computation of partial derivatives gives

\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}(x,y)=-{\frac{1}{2}}(1+x)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1+y)^{-\frac{3}{2}},\quad\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x,y)=-\frac{3}{2}(1+x)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+y)^{-\frac{5}{2}}.

The paralinearization in Zygmund spaces Lemma
A.7
gives

J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1=-\frac{1}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}+E.

Hence,

{\mathbf{P}}[(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})-1)\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]=-{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]+K,

since
T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}
is antiholomorphic, which vanishes after applying the projection
{\mathbf{P}}
, and

\|{\mathbf{P}}[E\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},E]\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|E\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

As for the last term of
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
, we write

{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]={\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]+K.

Setting

\mathcal{K}_{0,0}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]-\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]+\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}],

it is the leading term
of
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}
.
∎

Next we turn our attention to the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.

Lemma 4.3
.

The source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
have the representation

(4.10)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}R_{\alpha}+\tilde{G},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+\tilde{K},

where
(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})
satisfies the estimate

(4.11)

\|(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}.

Proof.

We begin with the analysis for
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
.
We compute using (
A.2
), (
A.6
),

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|Y\|_{C^{1+\epsilon}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|r\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}b\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},b)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\|b\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\|b\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}])\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|T_{\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}b_{\alpha}-{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha}}w\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{1}}\|b\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

All terms except
-{\mathbf{P}}T_{w}b_{\alpha}
can be put into
\tilde{G}
.
For this term, we write

{\mathbf{P}}T_{w}b_{\alpha}=T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})R]_{\alpha}=T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G=T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}R_{\alpha}+\tilde{G}.

We continue with the analysis for
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},b)\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{r_{\alpha}}b\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|r\|_{H^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}\|b\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w_{\alpha}}(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+\|Y\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}})\|T_{w_{\alpha}}(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w_{\alpha},c)\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}\|c\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}T_{w}a\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,a)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|a\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{g+a}wY\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi((g+a)w,Y)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(g+\|a\|_{L^{\infty}})\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|Y\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}(1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1})\|w\|_{H^{1}},

\displaystyle\|T_{1-Y}T_{c}w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}+\|T_{1-Y}T_{{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}}w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|c\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

For
T_{Lw}Y
and
\Pi(Lw,Y)
terms, we expand
Lw
and use (
A.2
), (
A.6
) to shift the derivative for the leading term
\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}w_{\alpha})
.

\displaystyle\|T_{Lw}Y\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}w_{\alpha})}Y\|_{L^{2}}+\|T_{cw_{\alpha}}Y\|_{L^{2}}+\|T_{T_{{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}}w}Y\|_{L^{2}}+\|T_{T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}}Y\|_{L^{2}}+\|T_{\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha})}Y\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\|Y_{\alpha}\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}+\|c\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\|Y\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}+\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|c\|_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\|Y\|_{C^{1}_{*}}\|\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2}\|w\|_{H^{1}}.

The estimate for
\Pi(Lw,Y)
term is similar.
Hence, all terms except
-T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c
can be absorbed into
\tilde{K}
.
For this term, we use the representation for
{\mathbf{P}}c
(
2.11
) to write

\displaystyle T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c

\displaystyle=T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}+T_{1-Y}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}c=T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}+\tilde{K}

\displaystyle=-iT_{1-Y}T_{w}(T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})_{\alpha}+K=-iT_{1-Y}T_{w}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+\tilde{K}

\displaystyle=-iT_{1-Y}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+K=-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+\tilde{K}.

Here on the last line we use para-products (
A.28
) and para-commutator (
A.26
) to simplify the para-coefficient.
∎

Putting the above two lemmas for the source terms together, we obtain the following formulas for the para-material derivatives of
(w,r)
:

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose
(w,r)
solve the paradifferential linearized equations (
4.4
), then we have the formulas

(4.12)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}w=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})+G_{2}=:G_{1}+G_{2}&\\
T_{D_{t}}r=-iT_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-Y)}(w_{\alpha\alpha}-T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+K_{2}=:K_{1}+K_{2},&\end{array}\right.

where
(G_{2},K_{2})
satisfy the estimate

\|(G_{2},K_{2})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}},

and
(G_{1},K_{1})
satisfy the linear bound

\|(G_{1},K_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}.

Proof.

For the para-material derivative of
w
, according to the conclusion of the previous two lemmas,

T_{D_{t}}w=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}R_{\alpha}+G_{2}.

Using (
2.4
) and (
A.4
), the term
-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w
can also be put into
G_{2}
.
This gives the formula for
T_{D_{t}}w
.

As for the para-material derivative of
r
, similarly, we have

T_{D_{t}}r=-iT_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+K_{2}.

It suffices to simplify the first term of
T_{D_{t}}r
.
We write,

T_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w=T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}\left(T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}}w_{\alpha}\right)=T_{1-Y}T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+K_{2}=T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-Y)}w_{\alpha\alpha}+K_{2}.

Hence, we get the formula for
T_{D_{t}}r
after applying the para-products (
A.27
).

The estimates for
(G_{1},K_{1})
are straightforward.
∎

To facilitate the normal form computation later, we also compute here expressions for the quadratic terms of the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
and
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.

Lemma 4.5
.

The quadratic terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[2]}=

\displaystyle-T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R-T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[2]}=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+K.

where
(G,K)
are perturbative terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

Proof.

According to the computation in Lemma
4.2
, it suffices to compute the quadratic terms of
(\mathcal{G}_{0,0},\mathcal{K}_{0,0})
.
We write

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}]

\displaystyle=-T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}[T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R]

\displaystyle=T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G,

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]

\displaystyle=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+K,

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha}]

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+K.

The remaining term in
\mathcal{G}_{0,0}
is cubic.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

The quadratic terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[2]}=

\displaystyle-T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w-\frac{1}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w,R)-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[2]}=

\displaystyle-T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}w

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}-\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha})+K.

where
(G,K)
are perturbative terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

Proof.

We begin with the computation for
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[2]}
.
Using the identity for
Y
(
2.7
),

{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})={\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+G.

We write for other terms

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}b-{\mathbf{P}}T_{w}b_{\alpha}=-T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},b)-\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha})=-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})-\Pi(w,T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}b_{\alpha}-{\mathbf{P}}b_{\alpha}}w={\frac{1}{2}}T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w-\frac{1}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w+G.

Other terms in
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
are cubic.

Then we compute the expression for
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[2]}
.
The first two terms are straightforward,

-{\mathbf{P}}T_{r_{\alpha}}b-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},b)=-T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+K.

Recall Lemma
2.6
for the leading term of
Lw
,

iT_{Lw}Y+i{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(Lw,Y)=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+\text{cubic terms}+K.

Using the paralinearization Lemma
A.7
,

J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1=-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)}{\mathbf{W}}-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-\bar{Y})}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}+E,\quad\|E\|_{C^{2}_{*}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}.

We can then write

\displaystyle-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+K,

\displaystyle-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}-1)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})+K.

For the five terms with
c
, we use (
2.9
) and (
2.11
) to compute

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}c

\displaystyle=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+K,

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},c)

\displaystyle=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha})+K,

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\Pi(w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+K,

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}T_{c}w_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}+K,

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}T_{{\mathbf{P}}c_{\alpha}}w

\displaystyle=iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}w+K.

The other terms of
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}
are perturbative and can be put into
K
.
∎

4.2.
{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}
energy estimate for the homogeneous paradifferential flow

In this subsection we prove Proposition
4.1
.
Consider the paradifferential systems with general source terms
(G,K)
that satisfy (
4.5
),

(4.13)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w=G&\\
T_{D_{t}}r+iT_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w-igT_{1-Y}w=K.&\end{array}\right.

Inspired by the linearized energy defined in
[
23
]
, we define the paradifferential linearized energy

E_{lin}(w,r)=\int-\Re(\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w})+\Im\left(w\cdot T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\right)+gw\cdot\bar{w}+\Im(r\cdot\bar{r}_{\alpha})+\Re\left(r\cdot\bar{r}\right)\,d\alpha.

Clearly,
E_{lin}(w,r)\approx_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}
.
We compute its time derivative

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)=-2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot w_{t}\,d\alpha+2g\Re\int w\cdot\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot r_{t}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int r\cdot\bar{r}_{t}\,d\alpha-\Re\int[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha+\Im\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}-w\cdot T_{(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha.

Note that using integration by parts,

\displaystyle-2\Re\int T_{b}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=2\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}(\mathcal{L}w)\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int[T_{b}\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}w\cdot\mathcal{L}\bar{w}\,d\alpha,

\displaystyle 2\Re\int w\cdot T_{b}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=-2\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int T_{b}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha,

we have the following identities:

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{b}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}+[T_{b}\partial_{\alpha},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha,

\displaystyle 2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=0,\quad 2\Re\int w\cdot T_{b}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=-\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha,

\displaystyle 2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=0.

We use these identities to replace the time derivatives by para-material derivatives.
Adding the above identities and applying (
4.13
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)=-2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{D_{t}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{D_{t}}w\,d\alpha+2g\Re\int w\cdot T_{D_{t}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r\,d\alpha+2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{D_{t}}r\,d\alpha-\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-\Re\int[T_{D_{t}},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+g\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+\Im\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}-w\cdot T_{(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{G}+i\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot K\,d\alpha+2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{1-Y}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-\Re\int[T_{D_{t}},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Im\int i\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w\,d\alpha+\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}\bar{w}-T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}w\,d\alpha+2g\Im i\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{1-Y}w\,d\alpha-2g\Re\int w\cdot T_{1-Y}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-g\Re\int w\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+g\Re\int T_{b_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-2\Im\int r\cdot T_{1-\bar{Y}}\mathcal{L}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+2\Re\int ig\bar{r}\cdot T_{1-Y}w\,d\alpha+2g\Re\int w\cdot\bar{G}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot G\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot K\,d\alpha-2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+\Im\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\ -w\cdot T_{(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}\bar{w}_{t},d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot\bar{G}-gw\cdot\bar{G}+\bar{r}\cdot K\,d\alpha+2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot K+T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot G-\frac{1}{2}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}w\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+2\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{1-Y}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Im\int i\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}\mathcal{L}w\,d\alpha+\Re\int\mathcal{L}w\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}-b_{\alpha}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+\Im\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}-w\cdot T_{(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha-2g\Im\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{1-Y}w\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Re\int[T_{D_{t}},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\cdot r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r)_{\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{G}_{\alpha}+gw\cdot\bar{G}-\bar{r}\cdot K\,d\alpha+2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot K+T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot G-\frac{1}{2}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}w\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-2g\Im\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{1-Y}w\,d\alpha+\Im\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}-w\cdot T_{(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int\left(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r\right)_{\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-\Re\int[T_{D_{t}},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha.

The first four integrals of
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
, namely,

\displaystyle 2\Re\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{G}_{\alpha}+gw\cdot\bar{G}-\bar{r}\cdot K\,d\alpha+2\Im\int\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot K+T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot G-\frac{1}{2}T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b_{\alpha}}w\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\Re\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{b}r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-2g\Im\int\bar{r}\cdot T_{1-Y}w\,d\alpha,

can be directly controlled by
(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}
.
For the fifth integral term on the right, we recall (
2.20
),

\partial_{t}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}={\frac{1}{2}}T_{J^{s}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{J^{s}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha}+E,\quad\|E\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}.

This integral can also be controlled by
(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}
.
The next two integrals have the cancellation,

\displaystyle-2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\cdot r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int\left(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r\right)_{\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Im\int T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\cdot r_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Im\int T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Im\int T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Im\int T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha.

They can also be controlled by
(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}
.
It suffices to find an energy correction to eliminate the last integral term
-\Re\int[T_{D_{t}},\mathcal{L}]w\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha
.
In other words, using (
2.21
), we need to find an energy correction
E_{cor}
such that
|E_{cor}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}
and its time derivative satisfies

\frac{d}{dt}E_{cor}=\int T_{\Re T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+O\left((1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}^{2}\right).

To construct this delicate energy correction
E_{cor}
, we first consider a simplified toy model and its linearized system

(4.14)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=-R_{\alpha}&\\
R_{t}=-i{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},&\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}w_{t}=-r_{\alpha}&\\
r_{t}=-iw_{\alpha\alpha}.&\end{array}\right.

Our aim is to find an energy
\tilde{E}_{cor}
such that for some constant
C
that depends on
{\mathcal{A}_{0}}
,

\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}_{cor}=\Re\int T_{R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+C(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}^{2}.

Our candidate for the energy correction is of the form

\tilde{E}^{3}_{cor}=\int A(R,w,\bar{r})+B(R,r,\bar{w})+C({\mathbf{W}},w,\bar{w})+D({\mathbf{W}},r,\bar{r})\,d\alpha+\text{complex conjugates},

where
A,B,C,D
are paradifferential cubic forms.
Here, we write
\mathfrak{a}(\xi,\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A(R,w,\bar{r})
, and the other three symbols are defined in the same way.
The time derivative of this cubic energy correction is given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}^{3}_{cor}

\displaystyle=\int A(R,w,i\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha})+B(R,-iw_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{w})+C(-R_{\alpha},w,\bar{w})\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\int B(-i{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},r,\bar{w})+C({\mathbf{W}},-r_{\alpha},\bar{w})+D({\mathbf{W}},r,i\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha})\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\int A(-i{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},w,\bar{r})+C({\mathbf{W}},w,-\bar{r}_{\alpha})+D({\mathbf{W}},-iw_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{r})\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\int A(R,-r_{\alpha},\bar{r})+B(R,r,-\bar{r}_{\alpha})+D(-R_{\alpha},r,\bar{r})\,d\alpha+\text{complex conjugates}.

Taking the Fourier transform of the integral and comparing the symbols for each cubic integral, the symbols
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d}
solve the algebraic linear system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{a}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{b}+\xi\mathfrak{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\xi\eta\zeta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\xi^{2}\mathfrak{b}-\eta\mathfrak{c}-\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{d}=0&\\
\xi^{2}\mathfrak{a}+\zeta\mathfrak{c}+\eta^{2}\mathfrak{d}=0&\\
-\eta\mathfrak{a}+\zeta\mathfrak{b}-\xi\mathfrak{d}=0,&\end{array}\right.

where the
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
is a non-negative smooth bump function defined in (
A.16
).
Using integration by parts, the derivative on the third factor is equal to the minus of derivative of the first two factors in the integral, which shows the symbolic relation
\zeta=\xi+\eta
.
This algebraic system has the solution

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}=\dfrac{-3\zeta^{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{b}=\dfrac{-\zeta(2\xi^{2}+3\xi\eta+3\eta^{2})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}=\dfrac{\xi\zeta(3\xi^{2}+3\xi\eta+2\eta^{2})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{d}=\dfrac{-\xi\zeta^{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2}}.

Since
|\xi|\ll|\eta|
,
\zeta\approx\eta
, so that at the leading order

\mathfrak{a}\approx-\frac{1}{6}\eta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}\approx-\frac{1}{6}\eta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}\approx\frac{1}{9}\xi\zeta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}\approx-\frac{1}{9}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Hence, adding the complex conjugates, the leading part of the cubic energy correction is given by

\tilde{E}^{3}_{high}=-\frac{2}{3}\Im\int T_{\Re R}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}-\frac{1}{3}T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{3}T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha.

And the remainder part has the form

E^{3}_{low}=\Re\int L_{0}^{lhh}(R_{\alpha},w,\bar{r})+L_{0}^{lhh}(R_{\alpha},r,\bar{w})+L_{0}^{lhh}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},w,\bar{w})+L_{-1}^{lhh}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},r,\bar{r})\,d\alpha,

where
L_{k}^{lhh}
is some cubic forms of order
k
such that the first component is at low frequency compared to the second component.
The energy correction
\tilde{E}^{3}_{cor}=\tilde{E}^{3}_{high}+\tilde{E}^{3}_{low}
removes the
\Re\int T_{R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha
integral for the model problem.

We now go back to the paradifferential system (
4.13
), which can be rewritten as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}w_{t}=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}-T_{b}w_{\alpha}-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{b_{\alpha}}w+G&\\
r_{t}=-iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}w_{\alpha\alpha}-T_{b}r_{\alpha}-iT_{(1-Y)(J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}})_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}+K.&\end{array}\right.

The original unknowns
({\mathbf{W}},R)
satisfy (
2.15
) and (
2.17
), which we recall here

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}{\mathbf{W}}_{t}=-T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}-T_{b}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\tilde{G}&\\
R_{t}=-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}-T_{b}R_{\alpha}+\tilde{K},&\end{array}\right.

where
(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})
satisfies

\|(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}_{*}\times C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}1+\mathcal{A}^{2}_{1}.

Compared to the model systems (
4.14
), the full systems are quasilinear rather than linear, and they also have lower order terms.

We define the cubic part of the energy correction to be
E^{3}_{cor}:=E^{3}_{high}+E^{3}_{low}
, where

E^{3}_{high}=-\frac{2}{3}\Im\int T_{\Re R}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}-\frac{1}{3}T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}w\cdot T_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{3}T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r\cdot T_{1-Y}\bar{r}\,d\alpha.

E^{3}_{high}
is the quasilinear modification of
\tilde{E}^{3}_{high}
so that it takes into account the quasilinear nature of the system.
For the bound for the energy, clearly we have

|E^{3}_{cor}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}.

Then we consider the time derivative of
E^{3}_{cor}
.
When the time derivative applies to a para-coefficient, the term is perturbative and is controlled by
(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}
.
When the time derivative acts on
w
,
r
terms, we can freely combine or switch the order of para-coefficients using the para-commutators or para-products rules in Lemma
A.9
.
As a consequence, the time derivative of
E^{3}_{cor}
removes the desired cubic terms.
Due to the lower order terms in the systems, the time derivative of
E^{3}_{cor}
also introduces extra quartic integral terms.
Fortunately, these quartic terms are perturbative after the cancellation.
For instance, using integration by parts,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{3}\Im\int T_{\Re R}(T_{b}w_{\alpha})_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}+T_{\Re R}w_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{3}\Im\int T_{\Re R}T_{b}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}_{\alpha}-T_{\Re R}w_{\alpha}\cdot T_{b}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\Re R_{\alpha}}T_{b}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{3}\Im\int(T_{\Re R}T_{b}-T_{b}T_{\Re R})\bar{r}_{\alpha}\cdot w_{\alpha}+T_{b}w_{\alpha}\cdot T_{\Re R_{\alpha}}\bar{r}\,d\alpha\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}^{2}.

In other words, for some constant
C
that depends on
{\mathcal{A}_{0}}
,

\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}=\int T_{\Re T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+C(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}^{2}.

Therefore, choosing energy functional

E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w,r):=E_{lin}(w,r)+E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)

gives the desired norm equivalence and energy estimates.
This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

4.3.
Normal form analysis for the balanced source terms

In this subsection, we compute the normal form transformations that eliminate the balanced part of the non-perturbative quadratic source terms of
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
in (
4.4
), and show that these balanced normal form transformations are bounded.
We will first compute the normal form transformations that remove the non-perturbative quadratic parts of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
Then we will perform the normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.

4.3.1.
Balanced quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

On account of the quadratic terms computed in Lemma
4.5
, we seek to show that there exist balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w_{1}^{bal},r_{1}^{bal})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{1}^{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{1}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{1}^{bal}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{1}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).
We consider normal form transformations as the sum of bilinear forms of the following type:

(4.15)

\displaystyle w_{1}^{bal}

\displaystyle=B^{bal}_{1}\left(\bar{w},T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+C^{bal}_{1}\left(\bar{r},T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}R\right),

\displaystyle r_{1}^{bal}

\displaystyle=A^{bal}_{1}\left(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+D^{bal}_{1}\left(\bar{w},T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+{\mathbf{W}})}R\right).

Direct computation gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{1}^{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{1}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r},{\mathbf{W}})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})-T_{J^{-1}}C^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r},i{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}B^{bal}_{1}(\bar{w},R_{\alpha})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}C^{bal}_{1}(i\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}\partial_{\alpha}D^{bal}_{1}(\bar{w},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{1}^{bal}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{1}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r},R_{\alpha})+iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}C^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r},R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}A^{bal}_{1}(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{bal}_{1}(\bar{w},{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}D^{bal}_{1}(\bar{w},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{bal}_{1}(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}})
, and similarly for other balanced bilinear forms.
In order to match the balanced source terms, one has the following algebraic system for the symbols:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{1}+\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{1}=-\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{1}+\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{1}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{1}=-\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}+(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{1}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{1}=\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\eta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}+(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{1}-\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\eta(\eta+\zeta)\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},&\end{array}\right.

where the symbol
\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)
is defined in (
A.17
) to select the balanced frequencies, and
1_{\zeta<\eta}
is the symbol for holomorphic cutoff.
The solutions of this system are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{3(2\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+2\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{1}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{(2\eta^{2}+\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{1}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{3\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{1}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{(8\eta^{2}-8\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{1}\approx\frac{1}{3}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{1}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{1}\approx-\frac{1}{3\zeta}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{1}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

From the leading terms of these balanced symbols, we get that using Lemma
A.8
, balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w_{1}^{bal},r_{1}^{bal})
are bounded in the sense that

\displaystyle\|(w_{1}^{bal},r_{1}^{bal})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.3.2.
Balanced quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})

In view of the computations for quadratic terms Lemma
4.6
, we aim to show that there exist balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w_{2}^{bal},r_{2}^{bal})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{2}^{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{2}^{bal}+\text{cubic higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,w)+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(T_{1-Y}\bar{R},w_{\alpha})-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{2}^{bal}+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{2}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,r_{\alpha})-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},w_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},w)

\displaystyle+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(T_{1-Y}\bar{R},r_{\alpha})-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w_{\alpha\alpha})+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha},w_{\alpha})+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).
We choose normal form transformations as the sum of bilinear forms of the following type:

(4.16)

\displaystyle w_{2}^{bal}

\displaystyle=B^{bal}_{2,h}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},w)+C^{bal}_{2,h}(T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R,r)+B^{bal}_{2,a}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w)+C^{bal}_{2,a}(T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{R},r),

\displaystyle r_{2}^{bal}

\displaystyle=A^{bal}_{2,h}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},r)+D^{bal}_{2,h}(R,w)+A^{bal}_{2,a}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r)+D^{bal}_{2,a}(T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)}\bar{R},w).

Then one can compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{2}^{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{2}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{bal}_{2,h}(R_{\alpha},w)-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{bal}_{2,h}(R,w_{\alpha\alpha})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{bal}_{2,h}(R,w)

\displaystyle+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}},r)-T_{J^{-1}}B^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}},r_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-1}}C^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},r)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}B^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},w)-iT_{1-Y}C^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R},w_{\alpha\alpha})+T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}D^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R},w)

\displaystyle+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r)-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r_{\alpha})+iT_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha\alpha},r),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{2}^{bal}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{2}^{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{bal}_{2,h}(R_{\alpha},r)+i\partial_{\alpha}^{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{bal}_{2,h}(R,r)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{bal}_{2,h}(R,r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}},w_{\alpha\alpha})+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}},w)-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{bal}_{2,h}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},w)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},r)+i\partial_{\alpha}^{2}T_{1-Y}C^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R},r)-T_{1-Y}D^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{R},r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}A^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w_{\alpha\alpha})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w)+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}D^{bal}_{2,a}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha\alpha},w).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{bal}_{2,h}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},r)
, and
\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{bal}_{2,a}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r)
.
Other symbols for balanced bilinear forms are defined similarly.
Taking the Fourier transform and matching the quadratic source terms on the Fourier side, the balanced holomorphic symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h},\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h},\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h},\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}
solve the linear system:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}-\eta\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h}+\xi^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h}=0&\\
\xi\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}=-(\xi+\eta)\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\xi\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}+(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h}+\eta\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}=-\eta\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\eta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}-(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h}+\xi^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}=(\xi+\eta)(\xi+\frac{3}{2}\eta)\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta).&\end{array}\right.

The expressions for balanced holomorphic symbols are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}(\xi,\eta)=\dfrac{(2\xi^{3}-9\xi^{2}\eta-16\xi\eta^{2}-9\eta^{3})\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)}{2\xi(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(15\xi^{3}+31\xi^{2}\eta+25\xi\eta^{2}+9\eta^{3})\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)}{2\xi(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(\xi^{2}+4\xi\eta+3\eta^{2})\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)}{\xi(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}(\xi,\eta)=\dfrac{(3\xi^{3}+12\xi^{2}\eta+11\xi\eta^{2}+6\eta^{3})\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta)}{2\xi(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,h}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,h}\approx-\frac{5}{4}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,h}\approx-\frac{1}{4\xi}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,h}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta).

Similarly, the balanced mixed symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a},\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a},\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a},\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}
solve the linear system:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}-\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a}=-\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a}+\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a}+(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}=\frac{1}{2}\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}-(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a}-\zeta\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}=\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}&\\
\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}-(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}=\frac{1}{2}\zeta(\eta+\zeta)\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},&\end{array}\right.

Then solving the system, these balanced mixed symbols are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{\zeta(8\eta^{2}-8\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2\eta(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{\zeta(2\eta^{2}+\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2\eta(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},
