\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{3\zeta^{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{\eta(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{\zeta(2\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+2\zeta^{2})\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}}{2\eta(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{bal}_{2,a}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{bal}_{2,a}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{c}^{bal}_{2,a}\approx\frac{1}{3\eta}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{bal}_{2,a}\approx\frac{1}{9}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

From the leading terms of these balanced holomorphic or mixed symbols, we get that using Lemma
A.8
, balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w_{2}^{bal},r_{2}^{bal})
are bounded in the sense that

\|(w_{2}^{bal},r_{2}^{bal})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

To conclude this subsection, we have shown the following result:

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose that
(w,r)
solve the linearized water wave system (
4.4
), then the normal form variables
(w_{NF}^{bal},r_{NF}^{bal}):=(w,r)+(w_{1}^{bal},r_{1}^{bal})+(w_{2}^{bal},r_{2}^{bal})
, where
(w_{1}^{bal},r_{1}^{bal})
are defined in (
4.15
) and
(w_{2}^{bal},r_{2}^{bal})
are defined in (
4.16
), satisfy the norm equivalence:

\|(w_{NF}^{bal},r_{NF}^{bal})-(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Moreover,
(w_{NF}^{bal},r_{NF}^{bal})
solve (
4.13
) with source terms
(G,K)
having no non-perturbative balanced quadratic terms.

4.4.
Quadratic normal form analysis for low-high source terms.

In this subsection, we take into account the quadratic low-high inhomogeneous source terms of
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
in (
4.4
).
These low-high quadratic source terms are divided into three parts: low-high quadratic source terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
where linearized variables are at high frequencies, the other low-high quadratic source terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
where linearized variables are at low frequencies, and low-high quadratic source terms of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
We will construct normal form transformations to eliminate each part of these low-high quadratic source terms.

4.4.1.
Normal forms for low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
part one

Here we show that there exist low-high quadratic normal
form variables
(w_{1}^{lh},r_{1}^{lh})
that can remove the part of low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
where linearized variables are at high frequencies, namely,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{1}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{1}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w+\frac{1}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{1}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{1}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}w+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

We choose normal form transformations as the sum of bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{lh}_{1}

\displaystyle=B^{lh}_{h,1}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},w)+C^{lh}_{h,1}(T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R,r)+B^{lh}_{a,1}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w)+C^{lh}_{a,1}(T_{J^{{\frac{1}{2}}}}\bar{R},r),

\displaystyle r^{lh}_{1}

\displaystyle=A^{lh}_{h,1}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},r)+D^{lh}_{h,1}(R,w)+A^{lh}_{a,1}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r)+D^{lh}_{a,1}(T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)}\bar{R},w).

Plugging these bilinear forms into the system,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{1}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{1}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{lh}_{h,1}(R_{\alpha},w)-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{lh}_{h,1}(R,w_{\alpha\alpha})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{lh}_{h,1}(R,w)

\displaystyle+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}},r)-T_{J^{-1}}B^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}},r_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-1}}C^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},r)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}B^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},w)-iT_{1-Y}C^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R},w_{\alpha\alpha})+T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}D^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R},w)

\displaystyle+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r)-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},r_{\alpha})+iT_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha\alpha},r),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{1}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{1}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{lh}_{h,1}(R_{\alpha},r)+i\partial_{\alpha}^{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{lh}_{h,1}(R,r)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{lh}_{h,1}(R,r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}},w_{\alpha\alpha})+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}},w)-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{lh}_{h,1}({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},w)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},r)+i\partial_{\alpha}^{2}T_{1-Y}C^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R},r)-T_{1-Y}D^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{R},r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}A^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w_{\alpha\alpha})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w)+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}D^{lh}_{a,1}(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}_{\alpha\alpha},w).

The low-high holomorphic symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1},\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1},\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1},\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}
for bilinear forms
A^{lh}_{h,1},B^{lh}_{h,1},C^{lh}_{h,1},D^{lh}_{h,1}
solve the linear system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1}-\eta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1}+\xi^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1}=0&\\
\xi\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}=-{\frac{1}{2}}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\xi\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1}+(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1}+\eta\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}=0&\\
\eta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1}-(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1}+\xi^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}=\xi(\xi+\eta)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),&\end{array}\right.

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.16
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The expressions for the holomorphic symbols are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(\xi^{3}+3\xi^{2}\eta+5\xi\eta^{2}+3\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(3\xi^{3}+15\xi^{2}\eta+16\xi\eta^{2}+6\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{(2\xi^{2}+5\xi\eta+\eta^{2})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(3\xi^{3}+3\xi^{2}\eta+\xi\eta^{2}+\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})\eta}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,1}\approx-\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,1}\approx-\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,1}\approx\frac{1}{18\eta}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,1}\approx-\frac{1}{18}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Similarly, the low-high mixed symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1},\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1},\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1},\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}
for bilinear forms
A^{lh}_{a,1},B^{lh}_{a,1},C^{lh}_{a,1},D^{lh}_{a,1}
solve the linear system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1}-\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1}=0&\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1}+\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1}+(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}={\frac{1}{2}}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)&\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1}-(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1}-\zeta\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}=0&\\
\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1}-(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1}-\eta^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}=\eta\zeta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).&\end{array}\right.

The solutions of the system are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{\zeta(\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+3\zeta^{2})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{(\eta-\zeta)(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{(2\eta^{3}-5\eta^{2}\zeta+8\eta\zeta^{2}-6\zeta^{3})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(\eta-\zeta)(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{3\zeta^{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(\eta-\zeta)(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{(2\eta^{3}-\eta^{2}\zeta+4\eta\zeta^{2}+\zeta^{3})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(\eta-\zeta)(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,1}\approx\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,1}\approx\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,1}\approx-\frac{1}{18\zeta}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,1}\approx\frac{1}{18}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle w^{lh}_{1}=-\frac{1}{3}T_{T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}}w+\frac{i}{18}T_{T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}r+\frac{1}{3}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}w-\frac{i}{18}T_{T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{R}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}r+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{lh}_{1}=-\frac{1}{3}T_{T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}}r-\frac{1}{18}T_{R}w+\frac{1}{3}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}r+\frac{1}{18}T_{T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)}\bar{R}}w+\text{lower order terms}.

Low-high quadratic normal form variables
(w_{1}^{lh},r_{1}^{lh})
are bounded in the sense that

\|(w_{1}^{lh},r_{1}^{lh})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.4.2.
Normal forms for low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
part two

Here we show that there exist low-high quadratic normal
form variables
(w_{h,2}^{lh},r_{h,2}^{lh})
that can remove the part of low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
where linearized variables are at low frequencies.
More precisely,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{h,2}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{h,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{h,2}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{h,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}T_{w}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

We choose normal form transformations as the sum of bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{lh}_{h,2}

\displaystyle=B^{lh}_{h,2}(w,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}})+C^{lh}_{h,2}(r,T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R),

\displaystyle r^{lh}_{h,2}

\displaystyle=A^{lh}_{h,2}(r,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}})+D^{lh}_{h,2}(w,R).

Plugging these bilinear forms into the system, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{h,2}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{h,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{lh}_{h,2}(w,R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{lh}_{h,2}(w_{\alpha\alpha},R)+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{lh}_{h,2}(w,R)

\displaystyle+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{lh}_{h,2}(r,{\mathbf{W}})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{lh}_{h,2}(r_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-1}}C^{lh}_{h,2}(r,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{h,2}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{h,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{lh}_{h,2}(r,R_{\alpha})+i\partial_{\alpha}^{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{lh}_{h,2}(r,R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{lh}_{h,2}(r_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{lh}_{h,2}(w_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{lh}_{h,2}(w,{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{lh}_{h,2}(w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}).

The low-high holomorphic symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta),\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta),\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta),\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta)
for bilinear forms
A^{lh}_{h,2},B^{lh}_{h,2},C^{lh}_{h,2},D^{lh}_{h,2}
solve the linear system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}-\xi\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}+\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}=0&\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}-\xi^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}=-(\xi+\eta)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}+(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}+\xi\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}=-\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)&\\
\xi^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}-(\xi+\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}+\eta^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}=(\frac{3}{2}\xi(\xi+\eta)+\eta^{2})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),&\end{array}\right.

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.16
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The expressions for the holomorphic symbols are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(9\xi^{3}+10\xi^{2}\eta+3\xi\eta^{2}-6\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta)=-\dfrac{(9\xi^{3}+19\xi^{2}\eta+19\xi\eta^{2}+9\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{3(\xi+\eta)^{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}(\xi,\eta)=\dfrac{3(2\xi^{3}+5\xi^{2}\eta+6\xi\eta^{2}+3\eta^{3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)}{2\eta(9\xi^{2}+14\xi\eta+9\eta^{2})}.

Since
|\xi|\ll|\eta|
, the leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{h,2}\approx\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{h,2}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{h,2}\approx-\frac{1}{3\eta}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{h,2}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle w_{h,2}^{lh}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{i}{3}T_{r}T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r_{h,2}^{lh}=\frac{1}{3}T_{r}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}+{\frac{1}{2}}T_{w}R+\text{lower order terms}.

Low-high quadratic normal form variables
(w_{h,2}^{lh},r_{h,2}^{lh})
satisfy the estimates

\|(w_{h,2}^{lh},r_{h,2}^{lh})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.4.3.
Normal forms for low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

Here we show that there exist low-high quadratic normal
form variables
(w_{a,2}^{lh},r_{a,2}^{lh})
that can remove the part of low-high quadratic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
Precisely, we show that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{a,2}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{a,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{a,2}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{a,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R+\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{i}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
4.5
).

We choose normal form transformations as the sum of bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{lh}_{a,2}

\displaystyle=B^{lh}_{a,2}\left(\bar{w},T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+C^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r},T_{J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}R),

\displaystyle r^{lh}_{a,2}

\displaystyle=A^{lh}_{a,2}\left(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+D^{lh}_{a,2}\left(\bar{w},T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R\right).

Plugging these bilinear forms into the system, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{a,2}^{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{a,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}B^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w},R_{\alpha})+iT_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}C^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+{\mathbf{W}})}\partial_{\alpha}D^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w},R)

\displaystyle+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r},{\mathbf{W}})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-1}}C^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r_{a,2}^{lh}+iT_{(1-Y)J^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w_{a,2}^{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r},R_{\alpha})+iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}C^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r},R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}A^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},{\mathbf{W}})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}B^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w},{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}D^{lh}_{a,2}(\bar{w},{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}).

The low-high mixed symbols
\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta),\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta),\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta),\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta)
for bilinear forms
A^{lh}_{a,2},

B^{lh}_{a,2},C^{lh}_{a,2},D^{lh}_{a,2}
solve the linear system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}+\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}=-\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)&\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}+\eta^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}=-\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)&\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}+(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}=\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)&\\
\eta^{2}\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}+(\zeta-\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}-\zeta^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}={\frac{1}{2}}\eta(\eta+\zeta)\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).&\end{array}\right.

The solutions of this system are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta)=\dfrac{3(2\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+2\zeta^{2})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{(2\eta^{2}+\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{3\zeta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2}},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}(\eta,\zeta)=-\dfrac{(8\eta^{2}-8\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)}{2(4\eta^{2}-4\eta\zeta+9\zeta^{2})}.

Since
|\xi|\ll|\eta|
, the leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{lh}_{a,2}\approx\frac{1}{3}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{lh}_{a,2}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{lh}_{a,2}\approx-\frac{1}{3\zeta}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{lh}_{a,2}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Consequently,

\displaystyle w_{a,2}^{lh}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{i}{3}T_{\bar{r}}T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r_{a,2}^{lh}=\frac{1}{3}T_{\bar{r}}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}-{\frac{1}{2}}T_{\bar{w}}T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R+\text{lower order terms}.

Low-high quadratic normal form variables
(w_{a,2}^{lh},r_{a,2}^{lh})
satisfy the estimates

\|(w_{a,2}^{lh},r_{a,2}^{lh})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

4.5.
Cubic normal form analysis

In this subsection, we compute the cubic normal form transformations to eliminate the non-perturbative cubic source terms of
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
in (
4.4
) as well as those produced by quadratic normal form transformations.

We consider each part of the cubic source terms:

•

The time derivative acts on the para-coefficients of the quadratic source terms such as
T_{1-Y}
,
T_{(1-Y)(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})}
, and so on.
This results in an additional
T_{R_{\alpha}}
or
T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}
.
In this scenario, we estimate
R_{\alpha}
or
\bar{R}_{\alpha}
in
L^{2}
, which introduces an
N_{s}
factor.
Furthermore, we estimate
w
or
r
in
L^{\infty}
or Zygmund spaces, and use Sobolev embedding to further bound them by
\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}
.
The cubic source terms of this type satisfy (
4.6
) as desired.

•

Cubic and quartic terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
and
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
These terms include the cubic term
-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{w}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}]
in
\mathcal{G}_{0,0}
, cubic terms
-T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}]-\Pi(w,{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}])
in
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
iT_{1-Y}T_{w}a+iT_{1-Y}\Pi(w,a)-iT_{aw}Y-i\Pi(aw,Y)
, and the cubic part of
i(T_{Lw}Y+\Pi(Lw,Y))
in
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}
.
These terms also satisfy (
4.6
) by estimating
w
in
L^{\infty}
and using Sobolev embeddings.

•

The sub-leading term of the time derivative acts on the main factors of balanced quadratic normal form variables, leading to additional cubic terms.
Cubic terms of this type always satisfy (
4.6
) as one can freely distribute derivatives for balanced paraproducts.

•

The sub-leading term of the time derivative acts on the main factors of low-high quadratic normal form variables, introducing non-perturbative cubic terms.
When this occurs at variables at low frequencies, the cubic terms satisfy (
4.6
).
Hence, we only need to consider the case when the sub-leading term of the time derivative acts on high-frequency variables of low-high quadratic normal forms.

Consequently, the non-perturbative cubic source terms are of the following type:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{[3]}=L^{llh}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},b,w_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R,b,r)+L^{llh}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},b,w_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{R},b,r),

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{llh}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},w,R_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R,w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w,R_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bar{R},w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{llh}(w,b,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(r,b,T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R)+L^{llh}(\bar{w},b,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(\bar{r},b,T_{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}R),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{[3]}=L^{llh}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},b,r_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(R,b,w_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},b,r_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)}\bar{R},b,w_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{llh}(T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+L^{llh}(R,w,R_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},w,{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+L^{llh}(T_{(1+\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})(1-Y)}\bar{R},w,R_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L^{llh}(r,b,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(w,b,R_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(\bar{r},b,T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+L^{llh}(\bar{w},b,T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}),

where
L^{llh}
are trilinear forms where each of its arguments has relatively low, low and high frequencies respectively.
These non-perturbative cubic source terms need
{\frac{1}{2}}
less derivative to satisfy the estimate (
4.6
).

To give an outline of how to compute the symbols for these trilinear forms, we follow the idea of Section 3.2 of
[
23
]
by using auxiliary variables.
Consider auxiliary variables
Z_{\pm}
and
z_{\pm}
defined by

Z_{\pm}:=R\pm i|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathbf{W}},\quad z_{\pm}:=r\pm i|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}w.

Z_{\pm}
and
z_{\pm}
solve the scalar equations

\partial_{t}Z_{\pm}=\pm i|D|^{\frac{3}{2}}Z_{\pm}+\text{lower order terms},\quad\partial_{t}z_{\pm}=\pm i|D|^{\frac{3}{2}}z_{\pm}+\text{lower order terms}.

Setting the non-perturbative cubic source term

\mathcal{Z}_{[3]}^{\pm}=\mathcal{K}_{[3]}\pm i|D|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{G}_{[3]}=\sum_{\pm}L^{1}_{\pm\pm\pm}(Z_{\pm},Z_{\pm},z_{\pm}),

we will need to find the cubic normal form transformation

\displaystyle Z_{[3]}^{\pm}=\sum_{\pm}L^{2}_{\pm\pm\pm}(Z_{\pm},Z_{\pm},z_{\pm})

such that
Z_{[3]}^{\pm}
solve the equation

(\partial_{t}\mp i|D|^{\frac{3}{2}})Z^{\pm}_{[3]}=\mathcal{Z}_{[3]}^{\pm}.

By arranging the frequencies of each variable and the outcome frequency, from small to large in absolute value, the frequencies
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}
satisfy (
3.9
), and the denominators of the symbols of trilinear forms
L^{2}_{\pm\pm\pm}
satisfy the non-resonance condition

|\xi_{1}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{2}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{3}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\pm|\xi_{4}|^{\frac{3}{2}}\neq 0.

Similar to the discussion in Section
3.4
, the symbols of trilinear forms
L^{2}_{\pm\pm\pm}
are bounded and the normal form transformation
Z^{[3]}_{\pm}
satisfy

\|Z_{[3]}^{\pm}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\|Z\|_{H^{s}}}(1+\|Z\|_{C^{1+\epsilon}_{*}}^{2})\|z\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Switching back to
({\mathbf{W}},R)
and
(w,r)
as in Section 3.2 of
[
23
]
and adding appropriate para-coefficients as quasilinear corrections, we obtain the cubic normal form variables
(w_{[3]},r_{[3]})
, which satisfy the estimate

\|(w_{[3]},r_{[3]})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

The extra quartic terms produced by
(w_{[3]},r_{[3]})
satisfy (
4.6
) because normal form transformations replace the leading term of time derivatives by sub-leading terms, and thus reduce
{\frac{1}{2}}
derivative so that quartic terms become perturbative.

At the end of this section, we use the paradifferential energy
E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}
for the linear paradifferential system constructed in Section
4.2
as well as all the paradifferential normal form transformations to finish the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
Define normal form variables

(w_{NF},r_{NF}):=(w,r)+(w^{bal}_{1},r^{bal}_{1})+(w^{bal}_{2},r^{bal}_{2})+(w^{lh}_{1},r^{lh}_{1})+(w^{lh}_{h,2},r^{lh}_{h,2})+(w^{lh}_{a,2},r^{lh}_{a,2})+(w_{[3]},r_{[3]}),

where each part of the normal form variables are constructed in the above subsections.
Setting

E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}(w,r):=E^{{\frac{1}{2}},para}_{lin}(w_{NF},r_{NF}),

then the energy
E^{{\frac{1}{2}}}_{lin}
satisfies the norm equivalence and the energy estimate in Theorem
4.1
.

5.
The proof of local well-posedness

In this section, we finish the proof of the the main result of this article, namely, the low regularity well-posedness Theorem
1.3
.
Recall that it was already shown by Alazard-Burq-Zuily
[
6
]
that the system (
1.5
) is locally well-posed in
{\mathcal{H}}^{s}
for where
s>\frac{3}{2}
(Note that the proof of Theorem
1.1
uses Strichartz estimate, and does not hold in the periodic case).
In the following, we will just consider
s
as a fixed Sobolev index
1<s\leq\frac{3}{2}
.

Our proof below follows the sequence of steps laid out in the expository article of Ifrim-Tataru
[
25
]
.
We will first establish the
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
bounds for regular solutions, with
\tau>\frac{3}{2}
.
Then, we use these regular solutions to construct rough solutions in
\mathcal{H}^{s}
.
More precisely, regular solutions are obtained by truncating the rough initial data in frequency, so that we get a continuous family of solutions, thereby estimating only a solution for linearized equations at each step.
Finally, we prove the continuous dependence on the initial data in
\mathcal{H}^{s}
.

Note that we can make use of the scaling (
1.6
).
By choosing the scaling parameter
\lambda
small enough, we can make the
\mathcal{H}^{s}
norm of initial data and the control norm
{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\ll 1
, at the price to turn the gravity
g
into
\frac{g}{\lambda^{2}}
.
For simplicity, we only give an outline of the proof below.
We refer the interested readers to Section 7 of
[
5
]
for the details of the proof, especially the definition and the use of
frequency envelopes
.

Outline of proof of Theorem
1.3
.

The proof of the theorem is divided into the following three steps:

(1)

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
bounds for regular solutions.

Suppose we have an
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
solution
({\mathbf{W}},R)
that satisfies the initial condition

\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\leq\mathcal{M}_{0}\ll 1,

for some small constant
\mathcal{M}_{0}
.
In order to show that there exists a time
T=T(\mathcal{M}_{0})
such that the solution exists in
C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{\tau})
and satisfies the bounds

(5.1)

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s})}<\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}_{0}),

(5.2)

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\tau})}\leq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{M}_{0})\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\tau}},

for constants
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{M}
that depend on
\mathcal{M}_{0}
,
we make the bootstrap assumption

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s})}<2\mathcal{M}.

By the bootstrap assumption and Sobolev embedding, the control norms satisfy

{\mathcal{A}_{0}},{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\lesssim\mathcal{M}.

Using the paradifferential modified energy estimates Theorem
1.2
and Gronwall’s inequality, we get the energy estimate

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}<e^{Ct}\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\lesssim e^{Ct}\mathcal{M}_{0}.

By choosing constant
\mathcal{M}
large and time
T
small enough, we get the bound (
5.1
).
Similarly, applying Theorem
1.2
for
\tau
and Gronwall’s inequality,

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\tau}}\lesssim e^{Ct}\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\tau}},

which gives the bound (
5.2
).
Therefore we have the
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
bounds for regular solutions up to time
T
.

(2)
Construction of rough solutions,
({\mathbf{W}},R)\in\mathcal{H}^{s}
.

We truncate the frequency the initial data
(W_{<k}(0),Q_{<k}(0))
and
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k}(0),R_{<k}(0))
at
2^{k}
.
We can then establish the bound of regularized initial data using frequency envelopes.
The corresponding solutions will be regular, with a uniform lifespan bound as shown in Step (1).
Here
k
can be viewed as a continuous parameter rather than a discrete parameter.
Then

(w^{k},r^{k})=(\partial_{k}W_{<k},\partial_{k}Q_{<k}-R_{<k}\partial_{k}W_{<k})

solve the corresponding linearized equations around
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k})
.
For the high-frequency part of the regularized solutions, we apply the energy estimates for the full equations Theorem
1.2
.
Next, using Theorem
4.1
for the linearized variables
(w^{k},r^{k})
, one can establish the difference bound
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k+1}-{\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k+1}-R_{<k})
in
\mathcal{H}^{s}
.
Summing up with respect to
k
, it follows that the sequence
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k})
converges to a solution
({\mathbf{W}},R)
with uniform
\mathcal{H}^{s}
bound in time interval
[0,T]
.
This further shows that the solution is unique in the sense that it is the unique limit of regular solutions.

(3)
Continuous dependence on the data for rough solutions.

We consider an arbitrary sequence
({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})(0)
that converges to the initial data
({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})
in
\mathcal{H}^{s}
topology.
Again by truncating the initial data at frequency
2^{k}
and obtaining the corresponding regular solutions, we get the sequence of solutions
({\mathbf{W}}_{j}^{k},R_{j}^{k})
, respectively
({\mathbf{W}}^{k},R^{k})
.
Due to the continuous dependence for the regular solutions which is proved in
[
6
]
, we have for each
k
,

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})-({\mathbf{W}}^{k},R^{k})\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{\tau},\quad\tau>\frac{3}{2}.

On the other hand, for the initial data, we let
k
go to infinity,

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})(0)-({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})(0)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{s},\quad\text{uniformly in }j.

With the help of frequency envelopes, we get the uniform convergence for the solution:

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})-({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{s},\quad\text{uniformly in }j.

We can again let
k
go to infinity, and conclude that

({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})-({\mathbf{W}},R)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{s}.

This shows the continuous dependence on the data for solutions in
\mathcal{H}^{s}
.

∎

Appendix A
Paradifferential calculus and related estimates

In this section, we list the definition of norms and recall paraproducts and paradifferential estimates we have used in previous sections.
On one hand, the normal forms are not explicit bilinear forms; they are expressed using multi-linear Fourier multipliers.
On the other hand, in order to get the paradifferential modified energy estimate, we need to work at the paradifferential level to rebalance the fractional derivatives.
Many of these definitions and estimates are relatively standard.
They can be found in for instance
[
5
,
8
,
7
,
42
]
or the textbooks
[
10
,
31
]
.

1.1.
Norms and function spaces

We recall the Littlewood-Paley frequency decomposition,

I=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}P_{k},

where for each
k\geq 1
, the symbols of
P_{k}
are smooth and localized at
2^{k}
, and
P_{0}
selects the low frequency components
|\xi|\leq 1
.

(1)

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, the non-homogeneous Besov space
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R})
is defined as the space of all tempered distributions
u
such that

\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}}:=\left\|(2^{ks}\|P_{k}u\|_{L^{p}})_{k=0}^{\infty}\right\|_{l^{q}}<+\infty

(2)

When
p=q=\infty
, the Besov space
B^{s}_{\infty,\infty}
becomes the
Zygmund space

C^{s}_{*}
.
When
p=q=2
, the Besov space
B^{s}_{2,2}
becomes the
Sobolev space

H^{s}
.

(3)

Let
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
,
1\leq r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq\infty
, then for any real number
s
,

B^{s}_{p_{1},p_{2}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow B^{s-(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{2}})}_{p_{2},r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}).

As a special case when
p_{1}=r_{1}=2
and
p_{2}=r_{2}=\infty
,

H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})\quad\forall s.

The Sobolev space
H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})
can be embedded into the Zygmund space
C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})
.

(4)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be the space of all functions such that
\partial_{x}^{j}u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
0\leq j\leq k
.
For
\rho=k+\sigma
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\sigma\in(0,1)
, we denote
W^{\rho,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the
space of all function
u\in W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\partial_{x}^{k}u
is
\sigma
- Hölder continuous on
\mathbb{R}
.

(5)

The Zygmund space
C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})
is just the the Hölder space
W^{s,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
when
s\in(0,\infty)\backslash\mathbb{N}
.
One has the embedding properties

\displaystyle C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}),\quad s>0;\qquad L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C_{*}^{s},\quad s<0;

\displaystyle C_{*}^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C_{*}^{s_{2}}(\mathbb{R}),\quad H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow H^{s_{2}}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad s_{1}>s_{2}.

1.2.
Paradifferential and Moser type estimates

Definition A.1
.

(1)

Let
\rho\in[0,\infty)
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.

\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the space of locally bounded functions
a(x,\xi)
on
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\})
, which are
C^{\infty}
with respect to
\xi
for
\xi\neq 0
and such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi\neq 0
, the function
x\mapsto\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(x,\xi)
belongs to
W^{\rho,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and there exists a constant
C_{k}
with

\forall|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad\|\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{W^{\rho,\infty}}\leq C_{k}(1+|\xi|)^{m-k}.

Let
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, we define the semi-norm

M^{m}_{\rho}(a)=\sup_{k\leq\frac{3}{2}+\rho}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\|(1+|\xi|)^{k-m}\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{W^{\rho,\infty}}.

(2)

Given
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R})
, let
C^{\infty}
functions
\chi(\theta,\eta)
and
\psi(\eta)
be such that for some
0<\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{2}<1
,

\displaystyle\chi(\theta,\eta)=1,\text{ if }|\theta|\leq\epsilon_{1}(1+|\eta|),\qquad\chi(\theta,\eta)=0,\text{ if }|\theta|\geq\epsilon_{2}(1+|\eta|),

\displaystyle\psi(\eta)=0,\text{ if }|\eta|\leq\frac{1}{5},\qquad\psi(\eta)=1,\text{ if }|\eta|\geq\frac{1}{4}.

We define the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{T_{a}u}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{a}(\xi-\eta,\eta)\psi(\eta)\hat{u}(\eta)d\eta,

where
\hat{a}(\theta,\xi)
is the Fourier transform of a with respect to the variable x.

(3)

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
, an operator is said to be of order
m
if, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, it is bounded from
H^{s}
to
H^{s-m}
.

We recall the basic symbolic calculus for paradifferential operators in the following result.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
\rho\in[0,+\infty)
.
If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{0}
, then the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
is of order m. Moreover, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a positive constant
K
such that

\|T_{a}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s-m}}\leq KM^{m}_{0}(a).

In Besov spaces, we have similar results for symbolic calculus.

Lemma A.3

(
[
42
]
)
.

Let
m,m^{{}^{\prime}},s\in\mathbb{R}
,
q\in[1,\infty]
and
\rho\in[0,1]
.

(1)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{0}
, then there exists a positive constant
K
such that

\|T_{a}\|_{B^{s}_{\infty,q}\rightarrow B^{s-m}_{\infty,q}}\leq KM^{m}_{0}(a).

(2)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, and
b\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, then there exists a positive constant
K
such that

(A.1)

\|T_{a}T_{b}-T_{ab}\|_{B^{s}_{\infty,q}\rightarrow B^{s-m-m^{{}^{\prime}}+\rho}_{\infty,q}}\leq K\left(M^{m}_{\rho}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{0}(b)+M^{m}_{0}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\rho}(b)\right).

In particular, when
q=\infty
, above symbolic calculus results hold for Zygmund spaces
C^{s}_{*}
.

When
a
is only a function of
x
,
T_{a}u
is the low-high paraproduct.
We then define

\Pi(a,u):=au-T_{a}u-T_{u}a

to be the balanced paraproduct.
For later use, we record below some estimates for paraproducts.

Lemma A.4
.

(1)

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
\alpha+\beta>0
, then

(A.2)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(a,u)\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(a,u)\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})}

(2)

Let
m>0
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.5)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.6)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{H^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.7)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{C_{*}^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.8)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})}.

Using the above paraproducts estimates, we get the following result.

Lemma A.5
.

(1)

If
s\geq 0
, then

(A.9)

\displaystyle\|uv\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{H^{s}},

(A.10)

\displaystyle\|uv\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\lesssim\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\|v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{C_{*}^{s}}.

(2)

Let a smooth function
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
satisfying
F(0)=0
.
There exists a nondecreasing function
\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that,

(A.11)

\displaystyle\|F(u)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\mathcal{F}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{H^{s}},\quad s\geq 0,

(A.12)

\displaystyle\|F(u)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\leq\mathcal{F}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}},\quad s>0.

(3)

Let
s_{1}>s_{2}>0
, then

(A.13)

\|uv\|_{C^{-s_{2}}_{*}}\lesssim\|u\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}.

Lemma A.6

(Commutator estimates
[
5
]
)
.

The following commutator estimates hold for
1<p<\infty
:

(A.14)

\displaystyle\|[{\mathbf{P}},f]\langle D\rangle^{s_{2}}g\|_{W^{s_{1},p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}+s_{2}}_{*}}\|g\|_{L^{p}},\quad s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0,

(A.15)

\displaystyle\|[{\mathbf{P}},f]\langle D\rangle^{s_{2}}g\|_{W^{s_{1},p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s_{1}+s_{2},p}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{0}},\quad s_{1}\geq 0,\;s_{2}>0.

Here we change the
BMO
spaces to Zygmund spaces as in Lemma 2.1 of
[
5
]
.
We also change the homogeneous spaces to non-homogeneous spaces.
Although no assumptions are made on
f
or
g
in this lemma, later it will be applied to
f
or
g
which are either holomorphic or anti-holomorphic.

Finally, we need the following result of paralinearization in Besov spaces.

Lemma A.7

(Paralinearization
[
10
]
)
.

Let
s,\rho>0
, and
F(u)
be a smooth function of
u
.
Assume that
\rho
is not an integer.
Let
p,r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
and such that
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
.
Let
r\in[1,\infty]
be defined by
\frac{1}{r}=\min\{1,\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}\}
.
Then for any
u\in B^{s}_{p,r_{1}}\cap B^{\rho}_{\infty,r_{2}}
,

\|F(u)-F(0)-T_{F^{{}^{\prime}}(u)}u\|_{B^{s+\rho}_{p,r}}\leq C(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{B^{\rho}_{\infty,r_{2}}}\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p.r_{1}}}.

We remark that this lemma also works for multivariable functions
F
.
We simply need to replace
F^{{}^{\prime}}
by partial derivatives of
F
.
See for instance Lemma
3.26
in
[
6
]
.
We will apply this paralinearization result with
p=r=r_{1}=r_{2}=\infty
, so that this is an estimate in Zygmund spaces.

1.3.
Paradiffential estimates for bilinear forms

In the following, we consider the estimates for the bilinear forms.
Let
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
be two non-negative smooth bump functions

(A.16)

\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\left\{\begin{aligned} 1,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{2}|\\
0,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{10}|\theta_{2}|,\end{aligned}\right.

(A.17)

\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\text{ when }\frac{1}{10}\leq\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{|\theta_{2}|}\leq 10\\
&0,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{2}|\text{ or }|\theta_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{1}|,\end{aligned}\right.

and such that
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})+\chi_{1}(\theta_{2},\theta_{1})+\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=1
.
For bilinear forms
B(u,v)
with symbol
m(\xi,\eta)
, we define the paradifferential bilinear forms:

•

Low-high part and balanced part of the holomorphic bilinear forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{lh}(u,v)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\chi_{1}\left(\xi,\eta+\xi\right)m(\xi,\eta)\hat{u}(\xi)\hat{v}(\eta)d\xi,

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{hh}(u,v)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\chi_{2}\left(\xi,\eta+\xi\right)m(\xi,\eta)\hat{u}(\xi)\hat{v}(\eta)d\xi.

•

Low-high part and balanced part of the mixed bilinear forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{lh}(u,v)}(\eta)

\displaystyle=1_{\eta<0}\int_{\eta=\zeta-\xi}\chi_{1}\left(\xi,\zeta-\xi\right)m(\xi,\zeta)\bar{\hat{u}}(\xi)\hat{v}(\zeta)d\xi,

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{hh}(u,v)}(\eta)

\displaystyle=1_{\eta<0}\int_{\eta=\zeta-\xi}\chi_{2}\left(\xi,\zeta-\xi\right)m(\xi,\zeta)\bar{\hat{u}}(\xi)\hat{v}(\zeta)d\xi.

These represent low-high and balanced paradifferential parts of the bilinear forms
B(u,v)
, respectively
{\mathbf{P}}B(\bar{u},v)
, restricted to the holomorphic class.
We will always assume that bilinear symbols
m
are homogeneous, and smooth away from
(0,0)
.

When the bilinear symbol
m
is homogeneous, we have the following direct generalization of Lemma
A.4
for bilinear forms, see Coifman-Meyer
[
14
]
, Kenig-Stein
[
28
]
, Muscalu
[
33
]
, and Muscalu-Tao-Thiele
[
34
]
.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
B^{\mu}(f,g)
be a homogeneous bilinear form of order
\mu\geq 0
as above.
For the balanced bilinear forms, when
\alpha+\beta+\mu>0
,
\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}=\mu

(A.18)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{hh}(f,g)\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\mu_{1}}}\|g\|_{H^{\beta+\mu_{2}}},

(A.19)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{hh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\mu_{1}}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{\beta+\mu_{2}}}.

For the estimates of low-high bilinear forms,

(A.20)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{H^{s-m}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{-m}}\|g\|_{H^{s+\mu}},

(A.21)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|g\|_{H^{s+\mu}},

(A.22)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{s-m}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{-m}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{s+\mu}},

(A.23)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{s+\mu}}.

Below we have the following para-commutator, paraproduct, and para-associativity lemmas where
f
and
g
are functions.

Lemma A.9
.

(1)

(Para-commutators)
Assume that
s_{1},s_{2}<1
,
s_{1}+s_{2}>0
then

(A.24)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}T_{g}-T_{g}T_{f}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|g\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}},

(A.25)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}T_{g}-T_{g}T_{f}\|_{C_{*}^{s}\rightarrow C_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|g\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}},

(A.26)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}T_{g}-T_{g}T_{f}\|_{C_{*}^{s}\rightarrow H_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{s_{1}}}\|g\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}.

(2)

(Para-products)
Assume that
s_{1},s_{2}<1
,
s_{1}+s_{2}>0
then

(A.27)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}T_{g}-T_{fg}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|g\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}},

(A.28)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}T_{g}-T_{fg}\|_{C_{*}^{s}\rightarrow C_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|g\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}.

(3)

(Para-associativity) For
s+s_{2}>0
, and
0<s_{1}<1
,

(A.29)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}\Pi(v,u)-\Pi(v,T_{f}u)\|_{H^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{H^{s}},

(A.30)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}\Pi(v,u)-\Pi(v,T_{f}u)\|_{C_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}}.

(4)

For
0<s_{1},s_{2}<1
,
s+s_{2}\geq 0
and
\bar{v}=\bar{P}\bar{v}
,

(A.31)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}P(\bar{v}u)-P(\bar{v}T_{f}u)\|_{H^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{H^{s}},

(A.32)

\displaystyle\|T_{f}P(\bar{v}u)-P(\bar{v}T_{f}u)\|_{C_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}}.

The proof of this lemma is identical to Lemma 2.4-2.7 in
[
5
]
.
We roughly sketch the idea of proof for
(1)
and
(2)
.
By orthogonality, it suffices to consider the case where the frequency of
u
is
\lambda
.
If both
f
and
g
are at frequency
O(\lambda)
, the estimates are direct.
If
f
is at frequency
O(\lambda)
, and
g
is at frequency
\ll\lambda
, then we rewrite the commutator,

[T_{f_{\lambda}},T_{g_{\ll\lambda}}]u_{\lambda}=[T_{f_{\lambda}},g_{\ll\lambda}]u_{\lambda}=\lambda^{-1}L(f_{\lambda},\partial_{x}g_{\ll\lambda},u_{\lambda}),\quad(T_{f_{\lambda}}g_{\ll\lambda}-T_{f_{\lambda}g_{\ll\lambda}})u_{\lambda}=\lambda^{-1}L(f_{\lambda},\partial_{x}g_{\ll\lambda},u_{\lambda}),

where
L
is a translation invariant trilinear form with integrable kernel.
These estimates follow by estimating the trilinear form.

Finally, we record here the para-Leibniz
rule.
Recall the definition of
para-material derivative
,

T_{D_{t}}:=\partial_{t}+T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}.

We then consider the four versions of para-Leibniz errors.
The first two are unbalanced para-Leibniz errors.

\displaystyle E^{p}_{L}(u,v)=T_{D_{t}}T_{u}v-T_{T_{D_{t}}u}v-T_{u}T_{D_{t}}v,

\displaystyle\tilde{E}^{p}_{L}(u,v)=T_{D_{t}}T_{u}v-T_{D_{t}u}v-T_{u}T_{D_{t}}v.

The other two are the balanced para-Leibniz errors.

\displaystyle E^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}\Pi(u,v)-\Pi(T_{D_{t}}u,v)-\Pi(u,T_{D_{t}}v),

\displaystyle\tilde{E}^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}\Pi(u,v)-\Pi(D_{t}u,v)-\Pi(u,T_{D_{t}}v).

With the above notation, the Leibniz error can be bounded according to the following lemma.

Lemma A.10
.

(1)

For the unbalanced para-Leibniz error
E^{p}_{L}(u,v)
, we have the bounds

(A.33)

\displaystyle\|E^{p}_{L}(u,v)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|u\|_{C^{{\frac{1}{2}}-\sigma}_{*}}\|v\|_{H^{s+\sigma}},\quad\sigma>0,

(A.34)

\displaystyle\|E^{p}_{L}(u,v)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|u\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}}\|v\|_{C_{*}^{s+\sigma}},\quad\sigma>0,

(A.35)

\displaystyle\|E^{p}_{L}(u,v)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}}\|v\|_{C_{*}^{s+\sigma}},\quad\sigma>0.

In the case
\sigma=0
the same bounds (
A.33
) and (
A.34
) hold for
\tilde{E}^{p}_{L}(u,v)
with
\sigma=0
.

(2)

For the balanced para-Leibniz error
E^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)
, we have the estimates

(A.36)

\displaystyle\|E^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|u\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}_{*}}\|v\|_{H^{s+\sigma}},\qquad\sigma\in\mathbb{R},\quad s>0,

(A.37)

\displaystyle\|E^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\|u\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}-\sigma}_{*}}\|v\|_{C_{*}^{s+\sigma}},\qquad\sigma\in\mathbb{R},\quad s>0.

In the case
\sigma=0
, we also have the same bound for
\tilde{E}^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)
.

The proof of this lemma is almost the same as Lemma
3.6
in
[
5
]
, we only sketch the proof here.
For the unbalanced para-Leibniz error, when
\sigma>0
,

E^{p}_{L}(u,v)=(T_{b}T_{\partial_{\alpha}u}v-T_{T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}u}v)-[T_{b},T_{u}]\partial_{\alpha}v.

For the first difference,
b
cannot be at the low frequency, so that

T_{b}T_{\partial_{\alpha}u}v-T_{T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}u}v=\sum_{j\leq l<k}\partial_{\alpha}u_{j}b_{l}v_{k}.

When
\sigma=0
, we write

E^{p}_{L}(u,v)=(T_{b}T_{\partial_{\alpha}u}v-T_{b\partial_{\alpha}u}v)-[T_{b},T_{u}]\partial_{\alpha}v.

Then the bound for the difference follows by using (
2.4
).
The bound for the commutator term follows from the para-commutator estimates (
A.24
), (
A.25
).
As for the balanced para-Leibniz error, we can write

E^{\pi}_{L}(u,v)=T_{b}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}u,v)-\Pi(T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}u,v)+T_{b}\Pi(u,\partial_{\alpha}v)-\Pi(u,T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}v).

The estimates are the consequence of applying para-associativity bounds (
A.29
), (
A.30
).
